<summary>This is the title page for "Filum Ariadnes," a 17th-century alchemical treatise that promises to guide readers away from common errors and toward the "true path" of the chemical arts. It identifies the author by his pseudonym, Heinrich von Batsdorff, and positions the work as an act of Christian charity during the hardships of the Thirty Years' War.</summary>
<keywords>Ariadne's Thread, alchemy, chemical discourse, Heinrich von Batsdorff, Christophorus Reibehand, Friedrich Gruner, 17th-century printing, Weingarten Monastery, alchemy errors, particularia</keywords>

### ***FROM THE REVELATION, final Chapter***
#### *verse 17.*

> Let him who is thirsty come; and let him who desires, let him
> take the water of life freely.
<note>original: "Qui sitit veniat, & qui vult, accipiat aquam vitæ gratis." This verse from Revelation 22:17 is frequently used in alchemical texts to liken the discovery of the Philosopher's Stone to a gift of divine grace or "living water."</note>

<term>Revelation</term> <note>original: "Apocalypsis"; the final book of the New Testament.</note>
<term>water of life</term> <note>original: "aqua vitæ"; in alchemy, this can refer to distilled alcohol or, more symbolically, the transformative solvent of the Great Work.</note>
<term>freely</term> <note>original: "gratis"; implying the knowledge is given by God without cost or merit.</term>
<term>thirsts</term> <note>original: "sitit"; representing the seeker's spiritual and intellectual longing.</note>

# Preface to the Impartial Reader.

Favorable Reader / this German discourse, which has been compiled by a **chemist** <note>original: "Chymico"; in 1636, the roles of "chemist" and "alchemist" were not yet distinct.</note> who has been highly practiced for a very long time / and has been observed through manifold **laboring** <note>original: "laboriren"; referring to practical laboratory work and experimentation rather than just theoretical study.</note> / is given and dedicated to you in print for your use and benefit / provided you yourself have a desire for the truth / not so that the God- and truth-loving **Author** might seek any worldly honor or a great name thereby / No / his heart / which has been well known to me for several years now / shows otherwise / nor so that he might bring great gain and wealth to himself through the **publication** of such **secrets** <note>original: "arcano"; refers to hidden alchemical knowledge or "arcana."</note> / which are unjustly composed by almost all the inexperienced and unlearned / but his goal is directed primarily to the glory of God / and / as mentioned above / to the benefit of his neighbor.

Although the said **Author** is unknown in **verbal colleges** <note>original: "Collegiis verbalibus"; a derogatory reference to academic institutions that focused on rhetoric and logic rather than practical science.</note> / and where one only holds much regard for **Aristotelian whimsies** <note>original: "Aristotelischen Grillen"; a common critique by early modern scientists against the traditional philosophical curriculum based on Aristotle, which they viewed as outdated and impractical.</note> / for **it is better to live for God and be unknown to the world,** <note>original: "melius est vivere Deo & ignotum esse mundo." This phrase reflects a common sentiment in mystical and alchemical circles that true wisdom is found in quiet devotion rather than public fame.</note>

<summary>The author introduces a "German discourse" on alchemy, emphasizing that the work is based on years of laboratory experience and intended for the reader's benefit and God's glory rather than personal fame.</summary>
<keywords>preface, chemist, laboratory work, secrets, Aristotelianism, spiritual devotion, neighborly love</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Preface to the Impartial Reader." It transitions from a critique of academic "Aristotelian whimsies" to a defense of the author’s practical experience and divine guidance. The catchword from the previous page "than" leads directly into the Latin quote at the top of this page.</meta>

than to live for the world, and be unknown to God:
<note>original Latin: "quàm vivere mundo, & ignotum esse Deo."</note>
Yet I know that he will reveal himself to the true **philosophers** as a **legitimate son of doctrine** <note>original Latin: "legitimum Doctrinæ filium"; a title given to one who has truly mastered the secrets of alchemy.</note>, whom God has willed to lead to the truth through His almighty hand of grace—miraculously through rocks, through fires, through a thousand perils <note>original Latin: "per Saxa, per ignes, per mille pericula."</note>—and bring to the light of day. And indeed, it requires not much commendation; the work praises the master himself. He has compiled such a work not merely from imaginings or from logical deductions—which many a man spins and ponders **demonstratively** <note>original Greek: "αποδειϰτιϰῶς" (apodeiktikos); meaning with absolute or certain proof.</note> in his **own little brain** <note>original Latin: "in suo cerebello."</note> from the writings of the ancient **naturalists** and true **Mercurialists** <note>Followers of the "Mercurial" or alchemical path.</note>—but has himself handled the coals, built his furnaces, observed the **degrees of fire** <note>original: "gradus ignis"; the four specific levels of heat required for alchemical transformation.</note>, and made himself well-versed in his noble **art of assaying** <note>The practical skill of testing the purity and composition of metals and minerals.</note> for the recognition of every mineral; until finally, after many recounted errors, through the heartfelt mercy of God, he reached the true **goal** <note>original Latin: "Scopo."</note> and the **Bride** for whom he had courted so long. And though he always dealt with the squint-eyed **Leah** instead of **Rachel** <note>A biblical reference to Jacob being tricked into marrying the elder sister Leah before he could marry his true love, Rachel. In alchemy, this symbolizes the "false" or "preliminary" results that often deceive the researcher before the true "Stone" is found.</note>, he has now succeeded. And I must confess that in all my life I have seen no clearer **writing** <note>original Latin: "Scriptum."</note>, in which it seems as if it were a **Compendium of the whole truth of philosophy** <note>original Latin: "Compendium totius veritatis philosophiæ."</note>, sincerely extracted from all sincere philosophers: and **proven by the fact itself** <note>original Latin: "ipso facto comprobatum."</note>; therefore, no one will regret reading it. But if someone is burdened with other groundless—

He describes the text as a definitive compendium of philosophical truth proven through actual laboratory experience.</summary>

<keywords>son of doctrine, degrees of fire, assaying, Rachel and Leah, practical alchemy, Mercurialists, compendium of truth</keywords>

[human] opinions, then even the bright light of truth will seem like darkness to him; especially since GOD, whose secret counsel is an **abyss and depth** <note>The German term "Ungrund" refers to the "groundless" or "bottomless" nature of God, a concept later made famous by mystics like Jacob Böhme to describe the divine before creation.</note>, does not wish to turn our darkness into light—and perhaps because of the world's great ingratitude, it is not yet time for such great wisdom (whose ruler and possessor is solely and uniquely God the Holy Spirit, **who is sevenfold, or rather, thousandfold in his gift** <note>original Latin: "qui septiformis, imò milleformis munere"</note>) to become common knowledge at this time. 

But to those who are in Christ Jesus, and whose conduct is conformed to Christ, and [who dwell] in heaven—that is, in the right, true Body, whose head is Christ—who have not yet drunk from the self-dug cisterns of the false philosophers, nor from the Babylonian Antichristian poison-cup, but from their hearts sigh solely and uniquely for both the blood of the Lamb and its prototype, the **universal tincture** <note>The "Universal Tincture" refers to the Philosophers' Stone in its liquid form, believed to be a supreme medicine for both the human body and "diseased" base metals.</note>: to them, along with me, may God (who alone is wise) open and enlighten heart, mind, and thoughts. 

May He grant that we all be of one mind, love one another, and seek not our own, but rather love to the honor of God and the service of our neighbor, as this author has done. May we explore and recognize His glory, omnipotence, and love out of Nature—which is the true Divine Power—and thus, surrendered to Him, one day with all the elect and the **Seraphim** <note>The highest rank of angels, traditionally associated with the fire of divine love.</note> may [praise Him]...

A musical term for a composition for three voices, used here to symbolize the Holy Trinity.</note>: Holy, Holy, Holy is God the Lord of **Sabaoth** <note>original: "Zebaoth." A Hebrew title for God meaning "Lord of Hosts" or "Lord of Armies."</note>, who is, and who was; to the **Alpha and Omega** <note>original: "A vnd O." The first and last letters of the Greek alphabet, signifying God as the beginning and the end of all things.</note> be praise and glory, now and in eternity, Amen. Lord Jesus, yes, come quickly.

### A Poem <note>original: "Carmen." A formal verse composition.</note> Dedicated to the Most Distinguished and Likewise Most Experienced Man in the Chemical Art <note>original: "Chymia." In the 17th century, this term encompassed both modern chemistry and the esoteric practice of alchemy.</note>

# Mr. HEINRICH
# von Batsdorff /
### sent to his FRIEND AND patron, not the least of them, for the sake of love and honor.

> AS Theseus once, when thrust into the Labyrinth's cave,
> Was not to be devoured as prey by the Minotaur; <note>In Greek mythology, Theseus navigated the maze of the Labyrinth to slay the Minotaur. The "labyrinth" was a common metaphor for the confusing and contradictory writings of alchemists.</note>
> And as he could not have departed safely from that tangled house,
> Had he not followed the threads of the loving Ariadne:
> SO, Heinrich, you teach what the Sun, <note>original: "Sol." The alchemical symbol for Gold.</note> what the pale Moon <note>original: "Luna." The alchemical symbol for Silver.</note>
> Might achieve, along with fleeing Mercury <note>original: "Mercurius." Represents quicksilver or the essential spirit of matter; called "fleeing" because of its volatility.</note> at the same time.
> You show how to avoid chemical <note>original: "chymicos." Relating to alchemical processes.</note> errors; blessed
> Is he who sees your writings, who approves and holds them fast.

->Yours,<-

->Most respectfully,<-

->M. J. C. H.<-

<note>The initials M. J. C. H. likely belong to a colleague or student of the author. "Heinrich von Batsdorff" was itself a pseudonym used by the official Christoph Reibehand.</note>

<summary>This page contains a dedicatory poem praising Heinrich von Batsdorff. It uses the myth of Theseus and Ariadne's thread as a metaphor for how Batsdorff’s writings guide the reader through the "labyrinth" of alchemical secrets involving Gold, Silver, and Mercury.</summary>
<keywords>Heinrich von Batsdorff, Alchemy, Theseus, Ariadne, Labyrinth, Sol, Luna, Mercury, Dedication, Poetry</keywords>

# THE AUTHOR’S
### Preface.

Beloved and favorable reader, many might wonder at this German discourse, especially among those who know my humble person and name, as to why I have dared to write so clearly and plainly about such a high secret <note>original: "arcano." In alchemy, an *arcanum* is a "great secret" or a mystery of nature, specifically the preparation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>; whereas otherwise the Philosophers <note>The author uses "Philosophers" to refer to historical alchemists, such as Hermes Trismegistus or Geber.</note> have commonly kept their mouths shut to the highest degree, and only after their deaths were their writings published—yet even then not purely and clearly, but according to their custom in the highest state of obscuration <note>original: "Verdunkelunge." This refers to the alchemical tradition of *trobar clus*, or "closed writing," intended to hide secrets from the uninitiated through allegory and metaphor.</note>, leaving out the most essential part or otherwise tying a hard knot, so that almost no one dared to draw any foundation <note>original: "fundament." The basic principles or practical starting point of the Work.</note> from them. Thus, I must indeed confess myself that, because I have written so clearly according to the old opinion, many will be troubled and think concerning this and my person that it is merely a gathered opinion <note>original: "opinion." Meaning a theory compiled from reading other books rather than one's own discovery.</note> and has never been experimented upon <note>original: "experimentirt." Proven through practical laboratory work.</note> by me—assuming that I am a poor fellow, not skilled in languages <note>A reference to the author not being a university-trained scholar fluent in Latin or Greek, which was the standard for scientific writing at the time.</note>, who has moreover discarded many beautiful things and, as it were, wanted to cook everything together into a mash in a single pot. But to this I

->answer<-

<summary>In this preface, the author justifies his decision to write a clear and accessible alchemical treatise in German, contrasting his plain-spoken approach with the intentionally obscure and "knotted" writings of earlier philosophers.</summary>
<keywords>Heinrich von Batsdorff, arcanum, alchemical secrets, plain language, laboratory experience, obscuration, German vernacular, alchemical philosophy</keywords>

--- Page 15 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Author's Preface" from the previous page. Batsdorff is explaining his personal journey from skepticism and frustration with the "obscure" writings of other alchemists to his eventual discovery of the true principles of the work through the study of minerals and metals.</meta>

->### THE AUTHOR’S<-
->### Preface.<-

<margin>A v</margin>

I answer briefly, that in all my life I never took it into my mind to treat anything of this glorious and highly praiseworthy science <note>original: "Scientz." In this context, the "Sacred Science" of alchemy.</note>, because I was always horrified whenever I read such books and could find no goal <note>original: "Scopū." From the Latin *scopus*, meaning a target or aim.</note>. Therefore, I almost despaired that the firm, locked gates would ever be opened to me, the film of ignorance removed, and that I should be admitted into the sanctuary <note>original: "Bedecksahl." An archaic term for a covered hall or secret chamber of mysteries.</note> of the secrets of nature. For, despite all my sharp and deep-thinking contemplations <note>original: "contemplationibus." Philosophical meditations or focused mental studies.</note>, I could never tangibly find or perceive any useful possibility; hence, I highly despised chemistry <note>original: "Chymiam." Referring here to the "Spagyric" or alchemical art.</note> in my heart, so much so that I intended to bid it farewell <note>original: "valediciren." From the Latin *valedicere*, to say goodbye or renounce.</note> entirely.

And although in my profession I gradually learned to recognize many kinds of minerals <note>original: "mineralia."</note> and metals <note>original: "metalla."</note>—though not without careful reflection—these instigated me anew to speculate and brought me to the point that, with a firm resolution and Divine help, I desired to observe the concordances of the philosophers <note>original: "philosophorum concordantien." The effort to find common ground or "harmony" between the often contradictory writings of ancient alchemists.</note>. Along with this, I well-considered my own and other people's known, manifold errors, and finally became so firm in my theory <note>original: "Theoria."</note> that I believed if such a thing could not be put into practice, the Stone <note>original: "lapis." The *Lapis Philosophorum* or Philosopher’s Stone.</note> would never be able to be made in all eternity. For through these manifold errors, I could recognize the causes of impossibility not nearly so well as I could through the knowledge of minerals—

<meta>catchword: lien (likely "metalien" or "materialien")</meta>

<summary>The author describes his initial despair and contempt for alchemy due to its obscurity, before detailing how his professional experience with minerals led him back to a theoretical breakthrough regarding the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, skepticism, minerals, metals, Philosopher's Stone, concordantia, theory, Heinrich von Batsdorff</keywords>

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous page, where the author was discussing his renewed interest in alchemy through the study of minerals and metals. This section concludes the "Author's Preface," with the catchword "zu/" (to/at) indicating the transition to the next page.</meta>

->### THE AUTHOR’S<-
->### Preface.<-

...from minerals and metals), yet it was all, beyond expectation, in vain; and it felt exceedingly difficult for me that I had made my intention known to no man, and could have no written or oral instruction from anyone, neither in the beginning, middle, nor end. However, at last, the bottomless and mercifully kind God, out of grace, miraculously delivered into my hand a starting material <note>original: "Subjectum." In alchemy, this refers to the base substance or "first matter" chosen to begin the Great Work.</note>—which required much effort and reflection to know where the beginning should be made, so that it might be wisely dismantled, and the **pure separated from the impure** <note>original: "purum ab impuro." A core principle of alchemy: isolating the essential spiritual or physical essence from its useless dross or waste.</note>, and so that other requirements might be brought forth without obstruction. I must confess that, in this initial **preparation** <note>original: "præparation." The chemical process of treating the raw matter to make it ready for the next stages of alchemy.</note>, I made not a few mistakes, which is why the desired outcome was long delayed. But through God’s blessing, I finally found myself—as reported hereafter—in the happy harbor of **Fortune** <note>original: "Fortunæ." The personification of luck or fate.</note> with the highest joy, for which I am bound to thank my God all the days of my life. I would now certainly see myself in a different state of renown, had the well-known, ruinous **state of war** <note>original: "Kriegswesen." Likely a reference to the devastating Thirty Years' War (1618–1648), which caused immense poverty and displacement across German lands.</note> not so painfully deprived me of this true **treasure** <note>original: "Schatz."</note>.

Regarding my **practical method** <note>original: "practicam." The actual laboratory procedures and recipes, as opposed to theoretical philosophy.</note>, that I reveal it so sincerely, I am driven to do so by both Christian love for my neighbor and by **sympathy** <note>original: "Condolentz." Meaning a shared suffering or concern for the plight of others.</note> for the overwhelming amount of fraud <note>The author refers to "the puffers" or fraudulent alchemists who tricked people with false recipes and gold-making schemes.</note> [perpetrated in this field].

<summary>The author recounts his eventual success in finding the "starting material" for alchemy after many failures and solitary struggles. He reveals that while war deprived him of his wealth, his Christian duty and frustration with alchemical fraudsters have moved him to share his true methods.</summary>
<keywords>Subjectum, purification, alchemical preparation, Thirty Years' War, alchemical fraud, Christian charity, Fortune</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Author's Preface." The author justifies his decision to publish his findings, asserting that his motives are religious and altruistic rather than driven by a desire for fame or wealth. He reflects on the loss of his alchemical "treasure" due to the hardships of war, expressing a pious resignation to God's will.</meta>

->### THE AUTHOR’S<-
->### Preface.<-

...to/ <note>This "to" continues the grammatical flow from the previous page's final sentence regarding the "state of war."</note> that I hope no one will complain or look askance at this kind labor of mine, but rather recognize that I have not shied away from writing so clearly and revealing the **mercy** <note>original: "Barmhertzigkeit." The author views his alchemical discovery as a gift granted by divine compassion.</note> I obtained—as if it had not happened to me—with glorious praise. I have no intention of falsely putting myself forward or seeking a **stinking honor** <note>original: "stinckende Ehre." A common early modern idiom for empty vanity or worldly pride that is offensive to God.</note>, for **God the Most High** <note>original: "Gott der Allerhöchste."</note> is my only ornament and honor. Much less do I desire money or goods, which I could otherwise easily obtain through useless communication; no, not at all. It is solely so that God’s works may be revealed and His holy name may be praised. For the rest, I have enough treasure in my heart and let myself be satisfied with little.

And although the troubled times and other great misfortunes would not or should not permit me to reach such a **jewel** <note>original: "Kleinot." In alchemical literature, the "jewel" or "treasure" is a standard metaphor for the Philosopher's Stone or the successful completion of the Great Work.</note> again, I will not murmur against my **Creator** <note>original: "Schöpffer."</note> because of it, but say with **Job**: *The Lord gave, and the Lord has taken away* <note>original: "Der HErr hats gegeben / der HErr hats genommen." A quote from Job 1:21, emphasizing the author's stoic faith despite his losses.</note>; if it is meant to be, and God wills it, I will surely find the time and opportunity to regain what was lost.

If, however, someone were not satisfied with this simple and sincere **truth** <note>original: "Warheit."</note> of mine, but thought that through oral or written conversation...

...from <note>Continuing the verse from the previous page: "blessed be the name of the Lord."</note> the name of the Lord. For anyone who allows himself to be fooled by sweet talk or empty promises, let him know that I, along with Bernhard <note>Likely referring to Bernhard Trevisan, a famous 15th-century alchemical author whom the writer views as a spiritual and professional peer.</note>, have vowed to God and Nature to let the matter rest entirely with these little treatises. I have placed a lock upon my mouth from this point forward, for I wish to refer everyone to this certain assurance: no one, whoever he may be, shall obtain anything more from me beyond this publication, whether through poisons or through gifts. And because I assure everyone most certainly and abundantly that I have omitted nothing necessary for this work, let him be faithfully advised to diligently repeat <term>repeat (repetire)</term> <note>In an alchemical context, this refers both to the mental repetition of reading and the physical repetition of laboratory processes, such as redistillation.</note> everything he has read, just like the clean animals that chew the cud, so that he may grasp one thing after another in due course.

As for my humble person, should anyone wish to take offense, that is up to them. I consider that the subject <note>original: "Subjectum." This refers both to the author as a person and the "subject" of the alchemical work itself.</note> may also be a stumbling block <note>original: "Stein der Ergerniß." A biblical allusion to a "stone of stumbling," suggesting that the truth is often rejected by those who think themselves wise.</note> to anyone; it matters little to me whether one judges <term>judges (iudiciret)</term> it as white or black. And even if my writings seem strange to the heads of "Master Know-it-alls," they will nonetheless have to let them stand upon the foundation of truth. 

However, as to why I keep my name somewhat concealed, let no one be suspicious, as if I shunned the light for that reason. Rather, this is done primarily upon the advice of distinguished people. It would also be ill-spoken of me, as a true Evangelical cross- and burden-bearer of Christ <note>A self-description identifying the author as a devout Protestant enduring the hardships and persecutions of the era, likely the Thirty Years' War.</note>, at this time—I, who previously...

### THE AUTHOR’S
### Preface.

...it may be that I have allowed myself to be driven and worn by many a dark wind, and have suffered all kinds of misery. I seek to hide myself in accordance with old German honesty, for my only purpose is to discover with what kind of spirit the <term>great works of God</term> <note>original: "Magnalia Dei." This refers to the wonders of the natural world and the divine secrets of alchemy.</note> will be received by this present, prevailing evil world.

Meanwhile, if one person or another knows me, or learns my name from my <term>writing</term> <note>original: "scripto."</note>, he will undoubtedly—as an honor-loving <term>Philosopher</term> and a true <term>Nicodemite</term> <note>original: "Nicodemit." Named after Nicodemus, who visited Jesus by night to keep his faith secret, this term describes a person who hides their true spiritual or scientific activities from a hostile or judgmental public.</note>—conceal me out of unfeigned love. He will cherish my <term>fellowship</term> <note>original: "Societatem." Likely referring to a secret or informal brotherhood of alchemists.</note> in secret, for which he may rest assured that he shall receive honor before all men, as well as my full gratitude whenever the opportunity arises.

Thus, along with my commendation to Divine protection, I wished to state this heartily and as a brother to every true <term>Son of Hermes</term> <note>original: "Filio Hermetis." A traditional name for an initiate of alchemy, honoring Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of the hermetic arts.</note>, etc.

# TO GOD ALONE
### THE GLORY.

When I consider how many noble and learned people have written about this high <term>Secret</term> <note>original: "Secreto." In this context, it refers to the secret of the Philosopher's Stone or the art of transmutation.</note>, I am struck with a kind of dread that so few of them <term>accord</term> <note>original: "concordiren."</note> and agree with one another; for whoever reads their writings finds that one is of this opinion, the other of that; this one makes a **dry Stone**, that one a **moist Stone** <note>These refer to the two main methodologies in alchemy: the "Dry Path" involving high heat and crucibles, and the "Wet Path" involving lower heat and liquid solutions.</note>; this one desires the **Air**, that one the **Water**; one says that such a noble **little Pearl** <note>original: "Pernlein." A metaphorical term for the *margarita philosophica* or the hidden essence/seed of the Stone.</note> is found in all things, while the other says no to it.

Although many people today intend to maintain their <term>opinions</term> <note>original: "opiniones."</note> or writings with stately <term>arguments</term> <note>original: "argumentis."</note> and to defend them with **Philosophical** maxims, nevertheless, <term>experience</term> shows...

<summary>The author begins Part One by expressing his frustration with the contradictory nature of alchemical literature, noting that previous masters disagree on fundamental methods and materials. He contrasts theoretical arguments and philosophical maxims with the actual results of practical experience.</summary>
<keywords>alchemical contradictions, Philosopher's Stone, Dry vs. Wet Path, secret, experience vs. theory, quintessence</keywords>

->of the Chemical Discourse.<-

...experience <note>Continuing from the previous page: "...nevertheless, experience shows..."</note> that these very people have found themselves most highly deceived and have shot furthest from the mark.

It is painful to lament that such distinguished people so mislead their **fellow Christians** <note>original: "Nebenchristen." This reflects the early modern emphasis on the moral responsibility one owes to their community and fellow believers.</note> with such writings, bringing them great harm, and helping to almost completely extinguish the small, still-flickering spark of this **noble art** <note>The "noble art" refers to Alchemy, often considered a divine gift that was being obscured by poor scholarship.</note>; and it is not unreasonable to ask what such people seek or intend to achieve with their writings? Their neighbor's benefit, God's glory, or an immortal name? But they achieve none of these things; rather, many a person—who otherwise might have reached the **truth** much sooner without their deceptive books and false **allegories**—is so misled that great wealth and noble time are squandered, and he, in turn, falls into great disgrace, mockery, and contempt. It serves God's glory even less, for they employ falsehood and falsely call upon the Most High as a witness to it, even though before His Divine Majesty...

-># First Part /<-

...nothing is more hated <note>Continuing from the previous page: "...before His Divine Majesty, [there is] nothing more hated..."</note> than lies; indeed, they bring about so much trouble that God’s gifts are shunned and held in contempt by everyone.

They do indeed achieve an immortal name, but it is of such a character that they have more reason to be ashamed of it than to rejoice in it. It is also easy to judge what kind of <term>judgments</term> <note>original: "judicia"; here referring to the legal or divine sentences passed upon writers.</note> fall upon such <term>authors</term> <note>original: "authores"</note> who pass off nothing but pure, palpable lies and <term>opinions</term> <note>original: "opiniones"</note> as the "purest milk of truth."

Oh, what a bad conscience they acquire! It will be difficult for them to answer for such deceit before the Judge of all things. They have no excuse; they cannot, when it comes down to it, claim they meant well, or that their work was consistent with the truth and that many learned people had written similarly. Here it becomes manifest how far <term>experience</term> <note>original: "Experientz"; practical, laboratory-tested knowledge as opposed to book-learning.</note> lies from mere <term>opinions</term> <note>original: "opinionibus"</note>.

Many a person certainly thinks: "Here the hidden truth is brought to light; here is the heart of the matter; <note>original: "hier ligt der Hund begraben" — literally "here the dog is buried," a German idiom meaning "this is the core of the issue" or "this is where the secret lies."</note> here he hears the lice cracking." <note>original: "allhier höre er leusen" — likely a variation of the idiom "hearing the lice cough," referring to someone who believes they have discovered a very subtle secret or are being exceptionally perceptive about hidden things.</note>

<summary>The author warns that lying about alchemical truths is a grave sin that brings "God's gifts" into disrepute. He contrasts the immortal but shameful reputation of fraudulent authors with the necessity of practical experience over mere theoretical opinion.</summary>

# of the Chemical Discourse. #

...medicine, as well as **transmutation** <term>transmutation</term> <note>The alchemical process of changing base metals into gold or silver.</note> might be comforted.

Because this is such a repulsive matter—one that makes the rich poor, the wise into fools, and the respectable into the most despised—who would not judge along with the common crowd that all **Chemical books** <term>Chymische Bücher</term> should be burned? According to the ancient written laws, such people should be tolerated in no community, and one should no longer hold the slightest regard for such things; especially because not only **private persons** <term>Privat Personen</term> lose their property through it, but even **Princes and Lords** are most highly deceived and betrayed.

...can hardly even call by name: many ruined, unlearned, and banished soldiers, merchants, bankrupts, worthless apothecaries, wandering students <term>Bachanten</term> <note>Literally "Bacchants," these were itinerant scholars or wandering students in the late Middle Ages and Renaissance, often associated with a rowdy or begging lifestyle.</note>, and court lackeys <term>Hofjunckern</term> who can no longer support themselves and have learned nothing other than how to deceive people and promise mountains of gold. # First Part /

[The sentence continues from the previous page:] has enlightened the eyes and turned [the seeker] away from all false paths.

However, so that the little spark of knowledge <term>spark of knowledge</term> <note>original: "Füncklein der Erkentniß"; a concept in mystical alchemy referring to a small, innate piece of divine light within the human mind that must be nurtured to achieve true understanding.</note> does not die out, but rather increases, it is highly necessary that such false paths be well observed; otherwise, it can easily happen that one allows oneself to be led astray again by the lovely, merry, and pleasant songs of birds <note>A poetic metaphor for the persuasive but hollow promises found in deceptive alchemical texts.</note>, and fails to find the single way that leads into the **Temple of Apollo** <note>original: "Apollinis Tempel"; Apollo, the sun god, represents the "Solar" goal of alchemy—the production of gold or the achievement of spiritual enlightenment.</note>, which is found in no other way than through the **grace of God**, and is attained through the unceasing **contemplation of Nature**.

And since I would like to make myself useful to the **Spagyric Republic** <term>Spagyric Republic</term> <note>original: "Republica Spagyrica"; this refers to the international community of "spagyrists"—practitioners of alchemy who focused on separating and recombining the essential components of plants and minerals for medicine.</note>, I first deemed it necessary to reveal several "broken" false paths on which I, along with many others, spent many years and traveled upon, yet achieved not the slightest thing, because they were nothing but **sophistry** and sweet, pleasant daydreams which should not be followed;

<margin>1. False path regarding Mars <note>original: "Marte"; the alchemical and astrological name for the metal iron.</note>.</margin>

against which everyone will know how to guard themselves in the future to the best of their ability. Therefore I will—

...I will first take up the **particulars of Mars** <term>particulars of Mars</term> <note>original: "Martis particularia." In alchemy, "Mars" is the name for Iron. "Particulars" refers to specific, limited recipes intended to produce a small amount of gold or silver, as opposed to the "Universal" work of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> and make them known to the lover of the Art, so that he does not allow himself to be misled by the manifold processes with which almost all books are filled—those which are transcribed by wandering charlatans <note>original: "Vaganten"; itinerant "alchemists" who traveled from town to town, often defrauding wealthy patrons with fake demonstrations.</note> as certain transmutations—but rather, through my sincere warning, may free himself from fraud and escape cunning deception while preserving his wealth and courage.

The lure used by such deceivers toward the futile yields of **Mars** begins with this lovely "pipe" <note>A metaphorical reference to a bird-call or flute used to lure someone into a trap; continuing the bird-song metaphor from the previous page.</note>: they say that Mars possesses an abundant and very red **fixed Sulfur** <term>fixed Sulfur</term> <note>original: "Fixen Sulphur." Sulfur was considered the "soul" or active principle of a metal. A "fixed" sulfur is one that has been stabilized so it does not evaporate or change when exposed to high heat.</note>, which far exceeds the **Sulfur of the Sun** <note>original: "Sulphur Solis." The Sun represents Gold; the author is saying these deceivers claim iron contains a "redness" even more potent than that found in gold itself.</note> because of its deep redness and its power to tint. Therefore, they claim one must only find a way for this sulfur to achieve "ingress" <note>original: "Ingreß"; the ability of an alchemical substance to penetrate and merge with a base metal to transform its internal nature.</note>. If this is done, they claim it will thereafter infallibly tint **the Moon** <note>Silver</note>—which they say lacks nothing other than such a fixed sulfur—into permanent **gold** <note>original: "aurum"</note>, either partially or totally. For this reason, the **Philosophers** <note>A term used for the ancient and respected authorities of alchemical tradition.</note> have assigned the crown of all "particular" works to the Sulfur of Mars, which...

# First Part

...which would be too extensive to recount entirely, and besides, it is already better known to everyone than they might like.

This is such a cruel and seductive error that I consider it one of the worst next to those involving **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurio"; the metal and planetary spirit of quicksilver, which was the focus of many other failed alchemical paths.</note>. One's ears itch intensely when one encounters such a beautiful **process** <term>process</term> <note>original: "process"; an alchemical recipe or sequence of operations intended to achieve a transmutation.</note> which, by all appearances, can be **carried out** <note>original: "elaboriren"; to work out or refine through laboratory labor.</note> with little cost, seems perfectly reasonable, and promises a magnificent **profit** <note>original: "proufit."</note>. 

Should a man not be fooled when he sees how Iron is such a cheap thing, yet in **solution** <term>solution</term> <note>original: "Solution"; the state of a metal after it has been dissolved in a corrosive liquid like "strong water" (nitric acid).</note> it displays itself like the most beautiful **Gold**? In the fire, is it not so **fixed** <note>In alchemy, a "fixed" substance is one that is stable and does not evaporate or fly away when exposed to high heat.</note> that it is not consumed, but rather **increases** <note>original: "augmentiret"; refers to the alchemical goal of "augmentation," where the weight or volume of a precious substance is supposedly increased.</note>? Understand this to be the **Saffron of Mars** <term>Saffron of Mars</term> <note>original: "Croco Martis"; a reddish-orange powder (usually iron oxide or rust) created by heating iron with sulfur or acid. Its golden-red color often deceived amateurs into thinking they had found the "Seed of Gold."</note>. 

Does **Mars** <note>The alchemical name for Iron.</note> not allow himself to be handled however one wishes? How easily is he (in the opinion of all those seekers) transformed into **Copper**? How, when **smelted** with **antimony** <note>original: "antimonio"; a brittle, metallic element used in the laboratory to purify gold and create various alloys.</note>, does he yield such a beautiful, pure **regulus** <term>regulus</term> <note>original: "Regulum"; the metallic "button" or heart of metal found at the bottom of a crucible after smelting. ...a beautiful **Star**? <note>original: "Stern"; likely referring to the "Starry Regulus of Mars," a crystalline structure that appears on the surface of refined antimony and iron, often seen by alchemists as a sign of success.</note> How quickly it allows itself to be **transmuted** <note>original: "transmutiren"; the alchemical process of changing one substance into another.</note> into its first essence—namely, into a sweet **vitriol** <term>vitriol</term> <note>original: "vitriol"; a term for metallic sulfates. Indeed, I have seen myself how the old **chemists** <term>Chymici</term> <note>original: "Chymici"; practitioners of alchemy and early chemistry.</note> were so "drowned" in **Mars** <note>original: "Martem"; Iron.</note> and placed their trust in it in such a way that they dared to affirm the **possibilities** <term>possibiliteten</term> by pledging their very lives.

How absolutely nothing can be accomplished with this thieving, roguish fellow <note>The author personifies Iron as a "bübischen Gesellen" to emphasize its tendency to "steal" or consume other materials in the crucible without yielding gold.</note>, and among all the recounted virtues, not one can be brought to the **result** <term>effect</term> <note>original: "effect"; the successful outcome of the alchemical work.</note> one had hoped for?

### First Part ###

...carried itself, as often happens, or the ingredients were mixed with **Gold**. Therefore, the clever and wise should consider: how could the **Body of Mars** <term>Corpus Martis</term> <note>original: "Corpus Martis"; the chemical substance of iron. In alchemy, metals were named after planets.</note>, which is subject to **corruption** <note>Meaning rust or oxidation; iron decays faster than other metals when exposed to the elements.</note> more than any other **Metal**, possibly grant to **Silver** such a **fixity** <term>fixitet</term> <note>The quality of being "fixed" meant the metal could withstand the intense heat of a furnace without evaporating or changing.</note> that it should become equal to the most noble body of **Gold**? How can this substance, which cannot keep itself stable against any element, bring **Silver** to such a quality? 

Have you never read the concluding speech of **Basilius** <note>Referring to Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist whose writings were highly influential in the early modern period.</note>, where he says: "Mark, mark this in conclusion: nothing weak can help another"? By this single **saying** <note>original: "dicto"</note>, all **particulars** <term>particularia</term> <note>Alchemical "particulars" were recipes or processes that claimed to change only a small amount of metal or produce a superficial transformation, rather than the universal "Great Work."</note> and **processes** <term>proceß</term> that do not proceed from the true **foundation** <term>fundament</term> and **Center** <term>Centro</term> are truly knocked over into a heap and rendered worth nothing at all—no matter what one might say of **iron salt** <term>eyfern Saltz</term> and its **Sulfur** <term>Schwefel</term>. 

Every being that cannot maintain itself can maintain another even less; thus the **Sulfur of the Sun** <term>Sulphur Solis</term> <note>The "Sun" is Gold. Its sulfur represents its essential combustible or "soul" principle.</note> is one thing, and the **Sulfur of Mars** <term>Sulphur Martis</term> <note>The sulfur or "soul" of Iron.</note> is another, as has <unclear>already</unclear> <meta>The German text ends with "allbereiß" and the catchword "reiß", likely a variant or split of "allbereits" (already).</meta>

# of the Chemical Discourse #

...already mentioned; many of them have held the **signed star** <term>Signatstern</term> <note>Referring to the "Star Regulus of Antimony," a crystalline metallic substance that forms a star-like pattern on its surface when cooled under specific conditions.</note> in high regard, and believed it to be nothing other than the purest **Mercury of Mars** <term>Mercurius Martis</term> <note>original: "Mercurius Martis"; the essential "liquid" or "spirit" of iron.</note>, which is particularly comparable to the **Mercury of the Sun and Moon** <note>original: "Mercurio Solis & Lunæ"; the alchemical essences of Gold and Silver.</note> and could easily be **coagulated** or **digested** into either **Gold** or **Silver**. Many thousands of **processes** <term>process</term> testify to this, which are supposedly based on this or 그 means; however—to speak of it briefly—this is all vain labor, through which effort, time, and expense are lost, because the **Starry Regulus** <term>Regulus Stellatus</term> has been viewed entirely as the wrong substance for this purpose.

Certainly, the **Alchemists** believe nothing else than that the **Regulus of Mars** <note>The metallic heart produced by smelting iron with antimony.</note> is separated from its superfluous **Sulfur** <term>Schwefel</term> through the help of **antimony**, so that the purest substance of **Mars** <note>Iron</note> betakes itself into the **Regulus**. But this is an error: if they were to take **equal parts** <note>original: "partes æquales"</note> of **antimony** and **Mars**, they would find their **Regulus** to be no purer; rather, it would be entirely vanished and found within a **dross** <term>Schlacken</term> <note>Waste material produced during the smelting of ore; the author argues the "pure" metal is actually being lost in the waste.</note>, so that neither **Mercury** nor **Regulus** can be detected anymore.

Now, the steel had cast off its flux and surrendered it into the slag. This life, which is a pure **fire** but now lay hidden as if dead, had to be reawakened and ignited again with **elemental fire**. He claimed that through twelve years of continuous **flaming fire**, the **spirits**...

<header>First Part</header>

<page-num>34</page-num>

...the **rite of fire** <note>original: "ritus ignis"; referring to the specific ritualized method or habitual application of heat in the alchemical process.</note> united and imprinted with the fire of iron in such a way that, afterwards, this supernatural fire could purify, **fixate** <term>fixate</term> <note>To "fix" a substance meant to make it stable under heat, preventing it from evaporating or changing further.</note>, and bring the metals to the degree of perfection.

This provided the occasion to examine the progress of the work, where it was discovered that through the six-year continuous **flux** <term>Fluß</term> <note>The state of being molten or liquid under heat.</note>, the **slag** <note>Waste matter separated from metals during smelting; the practitioner claimed this dross held the secret to the Philosopher's Stone.</note> had not changed in the least; instead, slag remained slag. And when some of it was applied to **silver**, it showed not the slightest potential; rather, one perceived what there was to hope for even if it had stood for a hundred years <note>That is, the experiment was a complete failure, and no amount of additional time would have yielded the desired transformation.</note>.

Whether one should now marvel more at the author and his great presumption, or at the long patience of said **Prince**, I leave to its place. Yet, His Princely Grace was not unaware that the author had let it be known to his good **drinking companions** <note>original: "Sauffbrüder"; literally "drinking brothers," implying the alchemist was a boaster who spoke more freely while intoxicated.</note>...

# Erster Theil/
## First Part

**corrosion** <term>corrosion</term> <note>original: "corrosio"; the destructive eating away of a substance by chemical action.</note>, which gnaws and devours **iron** sooner than **straw**, and causes much harm in the human body; whereas the **gold** prepared through the true **Philosophical Way** is as sweet as sugar, and is not unfairly called **Sugar of the Sun** <note>original: "Sacharum Solis"; an alchemical term for a sweet, purified substance derived from gold, often associated with the refined state of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>.

In vain do people attempt to demonstrate that **iron** can be infallibly **transmuted** into stable **copper** through **vitriol**, just as **Claveus** <note>Gaston de Clave, a 17th-century alchemist and physician who defended the possibility of transmutation.</note> intends to uphold his **foundations of transmutation** <note>original: "fundamenta transmutationis"</note> against **Erastus** <note>Thomas Erastus (1524–1583), a Swiss physician and theologian who famously attacked Paracelsian alchemy and denied that metals could change their fundamental species.</note>; he sets out the **procedure** and commands it to be worked upon, with the opinion of perhaps gaining as much benefit from it as otherwise through any other **particular** <term>particular</term> <note>In alchemy, a "particular" is a process that achieves a limited result—such as purifying a specific metal or creating a minor medicine—rather than the "universal" goal of the Great Work.</note>, for which reason one should not so entirely despise the good **Mars** <note>In alchemical symbolism, Mars represents the metal iron.</note>; if the philosophers did not defend their **transmutation** in any other way /

That **Mars** <term>Mars</term> <note>The alchemical name for iron, associated with the planet Mars.</note> is transformed into **copper** happens not because of its own inner quality, but for the sake of **Venus** <term>Venus</term> <note>The alchemical name for copper.</note>; however, it is a pale and poor transformation, because no more copper is produced than the **vitriol** <note>A sulfate mineral; in this context, "blue vitriol" or copper sulfate.</note> itself contains of the **substance of Venus**. Therefore, those err significantly who say they have seen **iron plate** dissolve and disappear in a water of **vitriol** and **salt**, while seeing the purest copper lying at the bottom <note>original: "in fundo"; referring to the sediment or precipitate at the bottom of a vessel.</note>. Likewise, those carry no weight who claim that in the mountains, clefts, and veins, **ironstone** is transmuted into copper by vitriolic water simply because iron and copper ore are found mined <note>original: "brechen"; a mining term referring to the way ore breaks or is extracted from the rock.</note> close together.

In truth, I wish to report something else, so that it may be clearly seen how **Claveus** <note>Gaston de Clave, a 17th-century physician and alchemist.</note>, with this argument against **Erastus** <note>Thomas Erastus (1524–1583), a Swiss physician and theologian who famously argued against the possibility of alchemical transmutation.</note>, has done too little for the cause, and the foundation of transmutation is very poorly

->erroneous<-

## Of the Chemical Discourse.

...with <note>The catchword "mit" from the previous page suggests the sentence begins "with transmuted..."</note> transmuted **Iron** <term>Mars</term> <note>original: "Martem"; the alchemical name for iron.</note> for a long time, but meanwhile fell into thought: why was more copper produced from one **vitriol** <note>A sulfate of a metal, usually iron (green vitriol) or copper (blue vitriol).</note> than from another? I could get no further than the idea that **Hungarian vitriol** <note>original: "victriolum ungaricum"; a naturally occurring copper sulfate found in Hungary, highly prized by alchemists for its perceived potency.</note> must be more powerful than the common sort. However, because their green color—one being more beautiful than the other—and the fact they both yielded very much the same **Spirit** <note>The acidic distillate or vapor produced by heating the vitriol.</note> caused me to anatomize the **dead head** <term>caput mortuum</term> <note>The solid residue left behind after a substance has been distilled or heated to exhaustion.</note>, I noticed a significant and thought-provoking difference in the substance. Therefore, I immediately dissolved one **loth** <note>A traditional German unit of weight, roughly 14.6 grams or half an ounce.</note> of pure, refined **copper** in a water made of vitriol and salt. Into this beautiful, blue, astringent **solution**, I placed two **loths** of pure **iron plate** and let it stand in the heat. Thus, the iron plates disappeared, and the water—which was previously green and sweet—became entirely white with a red film on top. At the bottom, however, lay much red copper, as beautiful as if it had been filed. After rinsing and drying, I weighed this; it was a little more than one **loth**.

<summary>The author describes a controlled experiment to prove that the "transmutation" of iron into copper is actually a chemical displacement, where copper already present in a solution precipitates out when iron is added.</summary>

Erster Theil/
->Part One<-

...mixed with **black flux** <note>original: "schwarzem Fluß"; a mixture of potassium tartrate and nitre used by alchemists as a reducing agent to recover metals from their ores or salts.</note> and boiled, I received one **loth** <note>A German unit of weight, roughly 14.6 grams.</note> of **copper** in full, without any loss. Thus I obtained my copper, which I had taken for the trial; on the other hand, the **iron** was **calcined** <term>calcination</term> <note>The process of heating a metal or mineral in air to reduce it to a powdery oxide (calx).</note>. At this I was greatly startled, because I realized that not the slightest bit of the iron had been **transmuted** <term>transmutation</term> <note>The alchemical belief that one base metal could be fundamentally changed into a noble one.</note>. This is well known enough to the experienced **Chemist**, but to the inexperienced, beginners, and common people, it is wondrous and unknown.

With minerals and metals, the nature of things is such that one precipitates the other, which many understand as a "loving and willing acceptance" <note>The author is critiquing the older alchemical metaphor where chemical reactions were described as "marriages" or "affinities" between metals.</note>, but this is false. Of the Chemical Discourse.
### Part One ###
<page-num>49</page-num>

If one dissolves silver in **nitric acid** <note>original: "aqua fort"; literally "strong water," the alchemical term for nitric acid, used to dissolve silver.</note> and precipitates it with **copper**, it is a similar process, just like with **Venus** <note>Alchemical name for copper.</note> and **Mars** <note>Alchemical name for iron.</note>. For just as none of the copper is transformed into silver during such a precipitation, the same occurs with the iron. The precipitation happens because the nitric acid, by means of its sharp **spirits** <note>In this context, "spirits" refers to the volatile, acidic vapors or active chemical agents within the liquid.</note>, corrodes the **body of the Moon** <note>original: "Corpus Lunæ"; the alchemical name for silver, associated with the moon due to its white luster.</note> and takes in as much as it can hold. Meanwhile, when the copper is also added to this **solution** <term>solution</term>, those same spirits attack the weak substance—namely, the copper—and wish to dissolve it. In contrast, the spirits become so weakened that they must let the more **fixed** <term>fixed</term> <note>In alchemy, a "fixed" substance is one that is stable and resistant to the action of fire or solvent, whereas "volatile" substances react or evaporate easily.</note> body fall—not out of "love" <note>Alchemists often used human emotions like "love" or "enmity" to describe chemical affinities or the way substances attract or repel one another.</note>, but out of necessity. This occurs in the same way with other metals and salts and can be tested. **Vitriol** <note>A sulfate salt; here referring to the metallic salt formed during the process.</note> is merely a salt; therefore, salt immediately precipitates silver and **gold**, and indeed all dissolved bodies. Because the salt dissolves easily in water, the heavy and fixed substance must of necessity fall down to the bottom.

In mining, there is a different circumstance. When salty waters sit or flow upon rich copper ores, they take on something from them and become quite greenish. Because of this, the copper ores—such as **azurite** <term>Lasur</term> <note>A vibrant blue mineral of copper.</note>, **loose ore** <term>Mulm</term> <note>A mining term for friable, earthy ore deposits.</note>, and **native copper**—eventually become very poor, because the copper is carried away in the water. By throwing in old iron, the copper is precipitated and recovered. Meanwhile, the iron is dissolved or consumed in the water, and no falsely imagined **transmutation of Mars into Venus** <note>original: "transmutatio Martis in Venerem"; the alchemical theory that iron (associated with the planet Mars) literally changes into copper (associated with the planet Venus).</note> is perceived. How then should **verdigris** and **Crocus Martis** **do** <note>original: "thun/"; the catchword for the following page</note> it?

...**central fruit of the gold and silver tree** <note>original: "tralische Frucht des Goldes vnd Silberbaums"; a central alchemical allegory where metals are viewed as living plants that must grow and ripen to perfection</note>, outside of which no lasting, **fixed** <note>fixe; stable under heat</note> color can ever be obtained. ...because of its abundance of **sulfur** and **salt**, it behaves almost like fire, consuming and turning to ash. Since it tends to merge with **lead** <note>original: "Bley"</note>, they have called it **fixed** <note>In alchemy, "fixed" refers to a substance that can withstand heat without evaporating or changing</note>. Yet, it actually possesses the highest degree of combustibility, more than almost any other substance, as experiments clearly demonstrate. 

Some have **calcined** <note>original: "Calcinirt"; heating a substance to a high temperature to reduce it to a powder or "calx"</note> and **sublimated** <note>original: "Sublimirt"; the process of heating a solid until it turns to vapor and then collects again as a solid</note> **Tin** <note>original: "Jovem" (Jupiter); the alchemical name for Tin</note>, so that a remarkable yellow sulfur arose. This sulfur—once its "wings were removed" <note>An alchemical metaphor for making a volatile substance "fixed" or non-volatile</note> and after a long **digestion** <note>The process of exposing a substance to gentle, continuous heat</note>—was meant to be added to **silver** in the forge to provide a rich yield. However, such labor failed the test, because this supposed **fixed sulfur** <note>original: "fixer Sulphur"</note> was found to have no equality with the sulfur found in Gold. 

It is just as impossible to produce the **sharpest vinegar** <term>acetum acerrimum</term> <note>A term for a powerful alchemical solvent, sometimes called the "Philosopher's Vinegar," believed to be capable of dissolving metals</note> from **mercury sublimate** <note>original: "Mercurio sublimato"</note> and the **ash of Tin** <note>original: "Calce Jovis"</note>. This solvent was supposedly able to quickly dissolve all solidified bodies and return them to the **first matter** <term>primam materiam</term> <note>The original, primitive substance from which all metals and minerals were thought to originate</note>. Because they did not know what the **first matter of metals** <note>original: "prima materia metallorum"</note> truly was, they achieved nothing with it. Alchemists otherwise deal with **Tin** <note>original: "Jovem"</note> the least of all—

<margin>5. False path concerning Mercury</margin>
Now follows the fifth false path concerning the alchemists’ **mocking-bird**, **Mercury** <note>The metal **Quicksilver**, often personified as a trickster or "mocking-bird" in alchemical lore because it mimics the properties of other metals and easily escapes the chemist through evaporation</note>; and although I perhaps should have maintained a different order according to how the **planets** <note>In alchemy, the seven metals were synonymous with the seven known planets; Mercury represents both the planet and the metal quicksilver</note> follow one another, it will matter little to the reader, for I have guided myself by my **labors** <term>laboribus</term> <note>His practical laboratory experiments and chronological work</note> as they followed one after another from time to time.

This fifth false path is not the least, but one of the very greatest, through which even at this hour very excellent people go astray and are shamefully deprived of their property <note>A reference to "puffers" or failed alchemists who bankrupted themselves buying expensive quicksilver for flawed experiments</note>. For **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"</note> himself knows how to masterfully adapt himself for the sake of greater deception, in that he poses and presents himself just as one wishes to have him. Because he knows that the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <term>Mercurius Philosophorum</term> <note>A theoretical, refined substance that alchemists believed was the true "spirit" of the metal, as opposed to the common "vulgar" mercury found in shops</note> is the primary work, and that without it no foundation exists—nor can even the smallest thing be achieved without him—and that he is the **prime matter of all metals** <term>materia prima omnium metallorum</term> <note>The fundamental substance from which all metals were believed to grow in the earth</note>, he finds this honor quite pleasing...

and I continued the <term>fixation</term> <note>original: *figirte*; the process of making a volatile substance stable so that it no longer evaporates when heated</note> for so long that the time and the wait became tedious for me. After this, it occurred to me that the <term>alchemists</term> used the **Mercury of Saturn** <note>original: *Mercurium Saturni*; a substance extracted from lead, or mercury specifically prepared with lead, which was thought to be "colder" and more stable</note>, which was supposedly better <term>cooked</term> <note>original: *coquiret*; processed or refined by heat</note> and not as <term>volatile</term> <note>prone to evaporating</note> as the common sort. ...be made so volatile through **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note>. Anyone who labors in such a way can easily become so "rich" that dogs and cats remain their only livestock; <note>A sarcastic German idiom implying that the alchemist will lose everything and end up in extreme poverty, with no valuable farm animals left.</note> this is best learned by those who think they can obtain their **Gold** and **Fool's Stone** <note>original: *Narrenstein*; a mocking play on the "Philosopher's Stone" (*Stein der Weisen*), suggesting the pursuit is a fool's errand</note> from **common mercury** <note>original: *ex Mercurio com:*</note>. One should guard against such works and avoid **layering** <note>original: *stratificiren*; an alchemical method of placing materials in alternating layers in a crucible</note> along with **wet wax-work** <note>original: *nassen Wachswercke*; likely referring to *ceration*, the process of repeatedly adding liquid to a substance until it reaches a wax-like consistency</note>; then one will not have to search behind their ears for the profit. <note>An idiom suggesting that the profit will be non-existent or impossible to find.</note>

This is certain: whoever possesses the true **more-than-perfect seed of Gold or Silver** <note>original: *plusquamperfectū semen Solis vel Lunæ*</note>, and uses it for **fermentation** <note>original: *fermentiret*</note> and **projection** <note>original: *projection*; the final stage of the Great Work, where a small amount of the alchemical medicine is "projected" or cast onto a base metal to transform it</note>, can indeed produce Gold or Silver from Mercury. Otherwise, everything is lost. I have set this down here with a sincere heart, so that this discourse is not taken for baseless babble, but is recognized as a well-founded and tested truth. To whoever perceives it as well-intentioned, may it be kindly granted.

The **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Mercurius Philosophorum*; the purified, "philosophical" mercury believed to be the true starting material for the Stone, distinct from the liquid metal found in shops</note> is quite another thing than **common mercury**.

# First Part /

[Mercu]ry is as much a metal as gold and silver. Just as gold and silver, when melted together, can neither help, give, nor take anything from one another, so too is there little to be expected from these **amalgams** <note>original: *amalgamationibus*; the process of mixing mercury with other metals to form a soft paste or alloy</note>. **Common mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius Com.*</note> is too weak to dissolve the **bodies** <note>original: *Cörper*; the solid metals being acted upon</note> and bring them into their primary essence; one also sees clearly that **amalgamation** does nothing at all but merely corrode the **body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note> and does not **resolve** <note>original: *resolvirt*; to break a substance down into its fundamental, liquid-like components</note> it. Even when the mercury is driven away by a small fire, the metal—whether gold or silver—remains as beautiful and shiny as it was before, without any loss of its weight, **fixity** <note>original: *fixitet*; the quality of a substance that remains stable and non-volatile when exposed to heat</note>, or other properties. Whoever takes this fundamental principle to heart and trusts in **experience** <note>original: *Experientz*</note> will never again be persuaded that a true **increase** <note>original: *augmentum*; the alchemical goal of multiplying the quantity of precious metal</note>, much less a **tincture** <note>original: *Tinctur*; the legendary substance used to transmute base metals into gold</note>, can be achieved through the amalgamation of **gold** <note>original: *Solis*; literally "of the Sun," the alchemical name for gold</note>, **silver** <note>original: *Lunæ*; literally "of the Moon," the alchemical name for silver</note>, and common mercury.

Having completed this **labyrinth** or maze <note>The author uses the "labyrinth" as a metaphor for the confusing and erroneous paths taken by misguided alchemists.</note>, I shall proceed to reveal several particular, erroneous deceptions—

<summary>The author explains that common mercury cannot truly transform or increase gold and silver because it only coats them without changing their fundamental nature. He dismisses simple amalgamation as an alchemical dead end and prepares to expose further fraudulent practices.</summary>

### First Part

...e forgiven; I will stay with what God, Nature, and **Vulcan** <note>original: *Vulcanus*; the Roman god of fire, used here as a personification of the alchemical furnace and the art of chemistry.</note> have taught me. However, I protest against those who have long since worn out their shoes on such matters—that this discourse is not intended for them, as they are already filled with nothing but great secrets and arts. For I know beforehand that the **Philosophers' Stone** <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum*; the legendary substance sought by alchemists to transmute base metals into gold and achieve immortality.</note> is much sooner to be achieved than such an old fool’s head, drowned in **opinions** <note>original: *opinionibus*</note>, is to be led onto the path of truth; especially since nothing but truth and simplicity is to be found herein.

To those people I dedicate and hand over this, my **precious jewel** <note>original: *Kleinoth*; a term for a treasure or highly valued possession.</note> collected for myself: those who are thirsty for the pure milk of truth and harbor a great loathing for the absurd, groundless, and inexperienced claims of many "great people," who dispute over the shell yet have never seen nor tasted the grain. ...despaired; those who have the desire and longing to walk toward the **Temple of Apollo** <note>original: *Apollinis Tempel*; Apollo was the Greek and Roman god of light and truth, often used by alchemists to represent the source of wisdom and the solar "Gold" of the spirit.</note> and to serve the noble, pure, chaste **Virgin** <note>A common alchemical personification for the "pure" state of Nature or the "Mercurial" substance before it is corrupted or worked upon.</note>: may they accept my simple discourse and carry it with them like a signpost. Indeed, let everyone take this **treatise** <note>original: *Tractat*</note> into their hands in abundance, so that they may recognize how the **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*; referring specifically to the "Hermetic Philosophers" or alchemists of old.</note> have brought the truth to light with such obscured words **allegorically** <note>original: *allegoricè*</note>, and how, on the other hand, the Most High has given such a noble **pearl** <note>original: *Perlein*; a common metaphor for the "Philosophers' Stone" or the hidden truth of God.</note> in such a lowly form to both the poor and the rich. ### Part One ###

In such a consideration, there is no need to ask whether I should boast or set myself up as one to whom God and **Fortune** <note>original: *Glück*; here used in the sense of divine providence or favorable destiny.</note> have granted this great **Good** in this world, or whether my writings in this **matter** <note>original: *passu*; Latin for a step, stage, or case.</note> are also to be trusted, because nowadays many false books exist in which such fantastical dreams are to be found. Everyone has the measuring-stone or **touchstone** <term>touchstone: a hard stone, like jasper or basanite, used to test the purity of gold and silver by the color of the streak they leave; metaphorically, a standard for testing truth.</term> in hand, so that he may **judge** <note>original: *judiciren*</note> and be a judge himself whether I am a true possessor of this **sanctuary** <note>original: *Sacrarii*; a sacred place or shrine where secret or holy things are kept.</note> or not. Even more so will those pass an impartial judgment who already understand something of the Art, and to whom the dear God has granted a portion of His grace—those who have not lost this **prize** <note>original: *pretii*; the reward or value of the alchemical pursuit.</note> through excessive pride, hate, envy, and insatiable greed. For I perceive that this **science** <note>original: *scientz*</note> is a true **gift of God** <note>original: *donum DEI*; a common alchemical tenet holding that the secret of the Stone is a divine revelation, not merely a chemical discovery.</note> and is obtained by no one unless God wishes to reveal it to him. ## Second Part

nothing can be compared—even though many fluid substances exist in the vegetable kingdom, such as wax and oil; in the mineral kingdom, <term>Salt of Tartar</term> <note>original: *Sal Tartari*; potassium carbonate, often used as a flux</note>, <term>Sal Ammoniac</term> <note>original: *Sal armoniac*; ammonium chloride</note>, <term>Sublimated Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurius sublimatus*; mercury chloride</note>, etc.; and in the metallic kingdom, tin, lead, bismuth, antimony, zinc, and the like—yet all of these do not agree with it <note>the alchemical tincture</note> in the slightest degree. 

A comparison or example can be given in this manner: when one lets a <term>hundredweight</term> <note>original: *Centner*; a heavy unit of weight, roughly 100-110 pounds</note> of lead flow in a molten state and throws a single <term>dram</term> <note>original: *Quintlein*; a small unit of weight, about 1/4 of a lot or roughly 3.6 grams</note> of gold or silver into it, the lead takes up the <term>body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*; the physical substance of the metal</note> to such an extent that, in the smallest substance of the lead afterwards, this dram of gold or silver can be found and identified during the <term>assay</term> <note>original: *Prob*; the process of testing a metal for its purity or precious metal content</note>. 

If this occurs in common metals so quickly and easily, why should one wonder that the fixed and fluid <term>medicine</term> <note>the Philosopher's Stone in its liquid or "tincture" form</note> should not also do the same? Even if all the requirements for your <term>Tincture</term> <note>the transformative substance that changes base metals into gold</note>...

...of a salty, penetrating nature and endowed with the powers of all four elements. ### Of the Chemical Discourse /

Heaven is the father and Earth is the mother, which reveals itself when Heaven is conjoined with Earth, and the child of our Sun and Moon is born. 

But many will say of this: Just as it is impossible to bring the Heavens down to Earth, just as little can the **Philosopher's Stone** <term>lapis Philosophorum</term> <note>The ultimate goal of alchemy; a substance capable of transmuting metals and prolonging life.</note> be attained. They claim that just as the former is impossible, so the latter is a falsehood. But to answer briefly: we have no need of the visible heaven and earth, but rather of their powers. # Second Part /

The Most High <note>Refers to God as the ultimate source of natural laws and alchemical power.</note> has placed into our **Heaven** all heavenly powers, and into our **Earth** all earthly powers, so that it requires nothing further, as we shall explain in more detail in further explanations. For now, we will briefly let it rest with these few indications of the properties of the true **Stone of the Ancients** <note>original: "Stein der alten." This refers to the Philosopher's Stone as described by the earliest masters of the art.</note>. Let everyone see whether his **labors** <term>labores</term> <note>The physical work of the alchemist in the laboratory.</note> are consistent with this or not, and whether his **principles** <term>principia</term> <note>The fundamental starting substances or theoretical foundations.</note> are so aligned that they are not perhaps impure and useless. For whoever takes good heed in this and **ponders** <note>original: "ponderiret." To weigh carefully, both physically and intellectually.</note> everything well will be able to have a complete **judgment** <term>judicium</term> as to whether he can hope for a desired outcome or not.

Secondly, as to what the **matter of the stone** <term>materia lapidis</term> <note>The *prima materia* or "First Matter" required to begin the Great Work.</note> might be, let everyone remember what countless opinions have been offered on this single point. Many hold that because this is a **universal medicine** <term>medicin Universalis</term> <note>A substance capable of curing all diseases and perfecting all things, even base metals.</note>, it should and must be found in all places and can be made from every thing; they argue that if this were not so, the name would have been given to it unjustly. Another defends the opposite view.

Each one imagined a **matter** <term>materiam</term> as he pleased and defended it as best he could. I have ultimately heard so much, especially from the most prominent, that in their opinion nothing in this **noble Work** <note>The "Great Work" (Ars Magna) of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note> should be named, much less—since it is **universal** <term>universalis</term>—could one thing be preferred over another.

Therefore, the philosophers desire only a single material of one essence when they say: Our Body and our Spirit, our Father and our Mother. [They] had knowledge of this mineral, but they were terrified by the wondrous effects and by the colors of all the world appearing within it, and did not reflect further on other miracles <note>original: *miraculis*</note>. Many a sensible man who followed the literal word has stopped where he should have begun; many a man has come so close that he held the **Lily** <note>A symbolic name for the white stage of alchemy (Albedo), representing the purification of the matter into a "white stone" that can transform metals into silver.</note> in his hands and recognized its virtue, yet despaired of its power which serves this Work.

This noble **white flower** is the one called by so many names, which is brought about through the art of the <term>separation of the pure from the impure</term> <note>original: *separation des puri ab impuro*; the fundamental alchemical process of refining a substance by removing its dross or "feces."</note>, but it makes fools of most people. Because they insist that the **King's bath** <note>A metaphorical term for the chemical solution or solvent used to dissolve and purify the "King" (often representing gold or the essential soul of the metal).</note> must be clean, they are greatly offended by the lowly appearance of the <term>subject</term> <note>original: *subjecti*; refers to the *materia prima* or the raw, base starting material of the alchemical process, which was often described as looking like common filth or black earth.</note> and cannot understand that the Most High—for the sake of evil people—covered the little pearl <note>original: *Pernlein*</note> lying within it with such a wretched gray smock and the greatest poison <note>original: *Gifft*; many alchemical starting materials, like antimony or arsenic ores, were highly toxic.</note>, and they therefore wanted to be wiser than the Creator Himself /

->A 5<-

<summary>The author explains that the "White Flower" of alchemy is often missed because it is hidden within a lowly, poisonous raw material that offends the pride of the worldly. He warns that many students of the art stop their work prematurely due to the "wretched" appearance of the substance.</summary>
