...intended, and they invented countless **solvents** <note>original: *menstrua*; in alchemy, a solvent used to dissolve solids.</note> so that they might accomplish the aforementioned **separation** and obtain the true **Philosophical Mercury** <note>The essential living spirit of matter sought by alchemists, distinct from common quicksilver.</note>, which Zacharius called the **Flower of the Sun** <note>original: *Florem Solis*</note>, and which [George] Ripley and others have named the **sperm or the Fifth Essence of our fetid earth** <note>original: *sperma vel Quintam essentiam terræ nostræ fætidæ*; the "Fifth Essence" (Quintessence) is the purest part of a substance, while the "fetid earth" refers to the raw, unrefined starting material.</note>.

But because their undertaking was a labor of their own invention, it had to come to nothing. Nothing foreign must be added to our **material** <note>original: *materia*</note>; as soon as that happens, it becomes impure and unfit for the Work. In itself, it possesses nothing superfluous; it can also be driven off entirely into smoke—just like common Mercury—which is the **Life of Metals**. And if even the smallest part of this is lost in the fire, it is impossible to unlock the **Gold** and bring it back to its original essence <note>original: *erstes Wesen*</note> with it, as the **Assembly** <note>original: *Turba*; referring to the *Turba Philosophorum* (Assembly of the Philosophers), one of the oldest and most influential Latin alchemical texts.</note> testifies. 

Therefore, in the beginning, this **mineral** <note>original: *Minera*</note> requires absolutely no foreign thing for its **dissolution** <note>original: *solution*</note> or **separation**, but does so solely through and in itself; from this, afterwards, with the help of **Vulcan** <note>The Roman god of fire, used here to represent the heat of the alchemical furnace.</note>, its fiery **balsam** breaks forth.

<summary>This page warns alchemists against using artificial solvents, arguing that the true Philosophical Mercury must be extracted from the mineral using its own internal nature and fire. It emphasizes that the substance is self-contained and that adding foreign materials or losing its volatile "spirit" in the fire will ruin the process of refining gold.</summary>

...so that it is formally presented as tangible and intangible, yet visible, and so that nothing more can be improved upon it. Therefore the **Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*; the master alchemists of history.</note> cry out: Our **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the Great Work.</note> **sublimes** <note>original: *sublimiret*; a process where a solid is heated into a vapor and then purified as it becomes solid again.</note> and **dissolves** <note>original: *solviret*</note> itself, and does everything by itself. They say that after the **Conjunction** <note>The union of the "male" and "female" principles of matter.</note> it is the easiest "women's work" and "child's play." Thus, they simultaneously reject all those who wish to employ great subtleties, manipulate the **Elements**, and bring about new **principles** <note>original: *principia*; the fundamental components of matter, usually identified by alchemists as Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt.</note>. 

For **Nature** has already placed into the hand of the **Son of Wisdom** <note>A traditional name for an enlightened alchemist who understands the secrets of nature.</note> a **material** <note>original: *materia*</note> in which the very purest substance—or (so to speak) the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *quinta essentia*; the highest and most refined element that transcends the four physical elements of earth, air, fire, and water.</note> of the Elements—and all three of the purest principles are to be found. On the other hand, in the **separation** performed by the **Sophists** <note>False alchemists or "puffers" who lacked true understanding and used illogical methods.</note>, a great **doubt** <note>original: *dubium*</note> arises: what was Nature actually supposed to have made from their supposed principles? It is an error when one does not wish to distinguish between the natures and keep them in **Order** <note>original: *Ordnung*</note>, since for the sake of order, GOD has set every single thing in its place, so that it [remains] therewith—

...should remain; Therefore it must cause one to pity when an old **know-it-all** <note>original: *Nasenweiser*; a derogatory term for someone who thinks they are wiser than they are, literally a "nose-wise" person.</note> with his opinion banishes so many honest people from the capabilities of Nature, so to speak, and instead leads them toward impossible, difficult things of which he has no foundation or understanding. Many a man boasts that he possesses the **Philosophical Moon and Sun** <note>original: *Philosophische Lunam vnd Solem*; the purified alchemical principles of Silver and Gold.</note>, and yet can prove such **golden-ness** and **silver-ness** <note>original: *aureitatem vnd argenteitatem*</note> neither by deed nor by the **sayings of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophorum dictis*; the authoritative writings of past masters.</note>, because, according to the opinion of the **Rosary** <note>original: *Rosarii*; likely referring to the *Rosarium Philosophorum* or "Rose Garden of the Philosophers," one of the most famous alchemical texts of the 16th century.</note>, no **Sulfur** <note>In alchemy, Sulfur represents the active, masculine, and combustible principle of matter.</note> is to be found under the sun that is like the **Sulfur of the Sun and Moon** <note>original: *Sulphur Solis & Lunæ*; the essential "heat" or "soul" found within gold and silver</note> (understand this as being because of their **fixity** <note>original: *fixitet*; the quality of a substance that allows it to remain stable and non-volatile even under the most intense heat of the furnace.</note>). Therefore, for lack of fixity, no one can call his Sulfur or **Mercury** <note>The passive, feminine, and fluid principle of matter.</note> an **Embryonic Sulfur or Mercury of the Sun or Moon** <note>original: *Sulphur oder Mercurium Solis vel Lunæ Embryonatum*</note>, much less **Elemental** <note>original: *Elementale*</note>; instead he must **doubt** <note>original: *dubitiren*</note> whether it is not rather an **arsenical smoke** and a **realgar-like sulfur** <note>Realgar is a naturally occurring arsenic sulfide mineral; here it refers to "false" or toxic sulfur that mimics the appearance of the true material but is chemically unstable and poisonous.</note>, which is far removed from our **red and white ferment** <note>original: *fermento*; the "leaven" or catalyst used to "ripen" the material into gold (red) or silver (white).</note>, as one sees enough of these days, where many [seek] now the **Essences** <note>original: *Essentias*</note> of **clay** <note>original: *Letten*</note>, now of **copper, iron, and...**

...of Tartar <note>original: *Weinsteins*; potassium bitartrate, often used as a solvent.</note> / often only a poorly-formed rainwater solvent <note>original: *menstruum*; a term for a chemical solvent believed to dissolve solids.</note> from nature, which, having sat upon a calx of gold <note>original: *Goldkalch*; gold reduced to a fine powder through heating or chemical action.</note>, extracted nothing, and by itself through the force of putrefaction <note>original: *vi putrefactionis*; the alchemical stage of decay or breaking down through warmth.</note> turned red; they cry this out to be a "potable gold" <note>original: *aurum potabile*; a legendary liquid gold medicine believed to cure all ailments.</note> and foist it upon people for great sums of money, where, when it comes to the test, one afterwards finds clearly in the result <note>original: *Aussticht*; the final appraisal or "cut" of the work.</note> how little of a fixed Solar Sulfur <note>original: *Solarischen Sulphur*; the permanent, non-volatile essence of gold.</note> such extractions actually contain.

With our practice, it is quite different; although our mineral subject of the stone <note>original: *subjectum lapidis mineralis*; the base material used to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note> carries within it the true Philosophical Mercury and Sulfur <note>The two primary principles of alchemy: Mercury representing the spirit or fluidity, and Sulfur representing the soul or stability.</note>, so that entirely by itself, without any addition or subtraction <note>original: *sine ulla additione vel diminutione*</note>, such a perfect universal medicine <note>original: *perfecta universalis medicina*</note> can be made from it, yet the "most general universal" <note>original: *universale generalissimum*; the most potent and concentrated form of the alchemical elixir.</note> is much higher still.

For this reason, the Philosophers commanded that the medicine be applied to gold and silver, so that the spirituality might thereby be fixed—

...when the precipitated gold is taken, the **spirits of vitriol** <term>spirits of vitriol; sulfuric acid or the acidic vapors derived from it</term> precipitate along with it, so that from one **quintlein** <note>a small historical unit of weight, approximately 3.7 grams</note> of gold, one often obtains two <note>The author is noting that the weight doubles because the gold is heavily contaminated with acidic salts</note>. Furthermore, such spirits cannot be washed away or rinsed out, but rather cause significant hindrances. With the silver, one handles it in the following manner: it is dissolved in **aqua fortis** <note>original: *aqua fort*; "strong water," or nitric acid</note>, then precipitated in a copper pan; the **lime** <term>lime; *calx*, the powdery metallic residue left after a metal has been dissolved or burned</term> is thoroughly **sweetened** <term>sweetened; *edulcoriret*, the process of washing a precipitate with distilled water to remove all traces of corrosive salts</term>, and set aside for use. However, for greater certainty—lest one encounters hindrance due to the corrosive spirits—it is more cautious to use **leaf gold and leaf silver** <note>original: *folia Solis & Lunæ*; literally "leaves of the Sun and Moon," the traditional alchemical names for gold and silver</note> instead of the dissolved gold and silver in this work. In this way, one is freed from all concerns, and the old verses retain their value:

> If you wish to unlock the barred castle,
> Then let it not vex you
> That you join the head to the tail,
> Thus you shall soon find the art complete. <note>This verse refers to the *Ouroboros* or the circular nature of the alchemical process, where the end must be joined back to the beginning to achieve the "Great Work."</note>

To give direction, I report that I have been greatly led astray by **Sendivogius** <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist and physician</note> and others, in that they say: one is the **matrix** <term>matrix; the "womb" or vessel in which the alchemical transformation takes place</term>, the second is the seed of the man, and the third is the seed of the woman. Others [say]: Take the **red servant** and the **white woman** <note>common allegories for gold (the masculine, fixed principle) and silver or mercury (the feminine, volatile principle)</note>. Some: add the volatile to the fixed and coagulate it according to the ancient custom, and thus you have the **Tincture of the Wise** <note>the legendary Elixir or Philosopher's Stone</note>. **Theoph** <note>Theophrastus Paracelsus, the influential 16th-century Swiss-German physician and occultist</note> says: Take the **blood of the red lion** and the **glue of the eagle** <note>original: *gluten Aquilæ*; "Red lion" often refers to gold or iron oxide, while "eagles" usually signify mercury or sal ammoniac</note> etc. After long reflection, and for the sake of certain and correct experience, I undertook two works together and mixed the **ferment of the Sun and Moon** <term>fermentum Solis & Lunæ; the specifically prepared essences of gold and silver</term>, as the perfect seed of the man and woman, with the **matrix** in proper proportion.

Afterward, I combined the **ferment of the Sun** <term>fermentum Solis; the gold-based catalyst</term> as the seed of the man with the **Philosophical Earth** <term>Philosophical Earth; the purified solid base or "salt" that remains after a substance is processed</term> as its mother or white woman. Thirdly, I conjoined the **Philosophical Mercury** <term>Mercurium; the "living" mercury of the alchemists, considered the soul of all metals</term> with the white **ferment** or **calx of the Moon** <term>Calce Lunæ; the powdered residue of silver after it has been dissolved or burned</term>, all under **Vulcan’s** <note>Vulcan was the Roman god of fire; in alchemy, this refers to the heat of the furnace</note>...

## Part Two

# of the Chemical Discourse.

...then, once the right path is made known, [He will] also grant grace so that the **augmentation** <note>original: "augmentation"; the process of increasing the quantity and potency of the Philosopher's Stone once it has been created.</note> may successfully follow; which, as I believe, cannot happen in any other way than through its own beginnings. Therefore, I will let this discourse on the preparation rest for now.

<margin>INSTRUMENTS <note>original: "INSTRUMENTA"</note></margin>

I provide the following brief report on the necessary instruments. The **Philosophers** <note>original: "Philosophi"</note> say our **Stone** <note>original: "Stein"</note> is a single **matter** <note>original: "materia"</note> and requires a single vessel and a single furnace. Thus, one must conclude that it truly cannot be otherwise. I myself have had no need for such **sublimation** <term>Sublimation; the process of heating a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, used for purification.</term>, **calcination** <term>Calcination; heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile parts or achieve oxidation.</term>, or **reverberation furnaces** <term>Reverberation furnace; a furnace where the heat is reflected or "reverberated" from the roof onto the material, keeping the fuel and material separate.</term>, **lazy Henrys** <term>Lazy Henry; also known as an athanor, this was a self-feeding furnace designed to maintain a constant, steady heat for long periods without constant human intervention.</term>, or other various **furnaces** <note>original: "furnos"</note>. Elaborate vessels are unnecessary, especially for those who possess the truth; a single **glass** <note>original: "Glaß"</note>—a glass or earthenware vessel in the shape of a round chamber or an egg <note>The "Philosopher's Egg" was a common name for the oval-shaped glass flask used in the Great Work.</note>—is quite enough for the beginning. It is true, however, that those who walk this path in a different way devise other vessels and furnaces for themselves...

<summary>The author concludes his discussion on preparing the Stone, emphasizing that the process relies on divine grace and natural progression. He begins a section on laboratory equipment, arguing that true alchemy requires only simple, singular tools rather than complex, specialized furnaces.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author’s discussion on laboratory equipment. # Second Part /

Because their undertaking is strange, they must also have strange instruments. However, it must be asked: if the **spirit of mercury**, **vitriol**, **of the sun** <note>original: "Solis"; gold</note>, **of May dew**, **of wine**, **of sulfur**, **of sal ammoniac**, etc., were the **solvents of the Philosophers** <note>original: "menstrua Philosophorum"</note>, what kind of instruments must the dear ancients have used for the **coagulation** <term>Coagulation; the process of thickening or solidifying a substance, often through heating or chemical reaction.</term> of such corrosive spirits, since the use of glassware did not yet exist? I am of the opinion that their earthenware pots would have absorbed such subtle spirits and quickly coagulated them sooner than they expected. Yet, from this consideration, anyone can easily deduce that their **dissolving water** <note>original: "aqua solvens"</note> must have been a "dry water" or spirit. Otherwise, it would have been impossible for the aforementioned dear ancients, for lack of glass or solid vessels, to have been able to coagulate or preserve the cited spirits; moreover, all those **Philosophers** <note>original: "Philosophi"</note> who ever considered a dry **solvent** <note>original: "menstrui"</note> would have been grossly mistaken if another **resolution** <term>Resolution; the act of reducing a body into its constituent parts or a liquid state; dissolution.</term> were possible.

<summary>The author argues that early alchemists must have used "dry" solvents because the earthenware vessels of the time could not have contained the highly volatile or corrosive liquid spirits used in later chemistry. This serves as a theoretical justification for the use of dry paths in the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

<keywords>instruments, solvents, spirits, glassware, earthenware, dry water, coagulation, resolution, ancients, history of chemistry</keywords>

And although some objections might be raised against this, I still hold to my opinion that I shall be permitted to trust my **experience** <note>original: "experientz"</note> and the righteous writings of the Sages further than perhaps someone else’s imagination. Furthermore, the oft-mentioned dear ancient true **Philosophers** <note>original: "Philosophi"</note> did not incur so much expense to build such beautiful, artistic furnaces and splendid **laboratories** <note>original: "laboratoria"</note> as is common nowadays; instead, they devoted themselves to a single small furnace in a quiet place and attended to it. The multitude of many furnaces indicates varied labors and different matters, whereas, on the contrary, all writings unanimously agree that our Art does not consist of the essence of many things, as **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy; the author refers here to the principle of "All is One."</note> clearly speaks thereof. This he should set in a place where one does not have to worry about the **danger of fire** <note>original: "Fewersgefahr"</note>, so that the Devil, as a hateful enemy, might not

**matter’s** <note>original: "materia"</note> hidden heavenly and terrestrial **Fire** <note>original: "Fewer"</note>, to which that Arab <note>Likely a reference to Geber (Jabir ibn Hayyan), the influential 8th-century polymath and alchemist.</note> gives his agreement in his treatise when he says: "Put our **Gold** and **Silver** into our **Fire**, and a dry **liquid** <note>original: "liquor"</note> will come of it."

And since two **fires** are mentioned here, everyone should carefully observe that common fire is far too weak to burn our **phoenix** <term>Phoenix; in alchemy, this mythical bird represents the soul of the matter undergoing transformation through fire, leading to the Philosopher's Stone.</term>, unless the other fire, hidden in the **matter**—which is kept excellently secret and called by wondrous names—does the best work. And it is certainly true that these two fires can do nothing at all without the other; rather, for a result to follow from both together, the natural fire must ignite the other and make it active. Second Part /
<meta>catchword: To be burned- [Latin: comburi-]</meta>

**internal** [fire] surpass [the external], otherwise all labor would be in vain, and the **volatile** <note>Substances that easily evaporate or escape when heated.</note> would be driven off by the **fixed**, or—because our **mass** <note>original: "massa"</note> is itself fluid—be brought into a state of flux <note>Meaning it melts or flows away prematurely.</note>, as has happened to me on various occasions, such that my entire work fell into ruin. Instead, the **fire** must be—as **Bernard** <note>Likely Bernard of Trevisan, a famed 15th-century Italian alchemist who emphasized patience and gentle heat.</note> says—a steady, digesting, constant **fire**, not too violent or hot.

One must not rush with the **solution** <note>The process of dissolving the solid "body" into a liquid state.</note> in the first degree; otherwise, if your **body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note> is not rightly dissolved, no proper or lasting **fixation** <term>Fixation; the process of making a volatile substance stable so it can withstand heat without evaporating.</term> of the volatile can follow. After the **solution**, it is necessary that the second degree be conducted, wherein the entire **mass** transforms into the form of a white, diamond-like powder. Before this occurs, however, very beautiful colors will appear in the glass as well as in the **matter** <note>original: "materia"</note>.

When this happens, and the whiteness changes so that a **peach-blossom color** <note>original: "Pfirsichblüt Farbe"; a specific color stage indicating the transition toward the final "red" stage of the Great Work.</note> begins to shine forth, the **fire** can then no longer be made too strong, nor can anything be spoiled; for the **Philosophers** <note>original: "Philosophi"; referring to the historical masters of alchemy.</note> testify to this and intend it when they say: **To be burned-** <note>original: "Comburi-"</note>

## Second Part /

<meta>catchword: curio-</meta>

The **Spirit** <note>original: "Spiritus"; the volatile, active principle of the substance.</note> has coagulated and no longer possesses its previous fluidity; nor can any further separation occur. Out of two things, one has been made, so that henceforth no element may divide them from each other. 

How? (someone might ask) are you not contradicting yourself? Should your **perfect mass** or **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original: "lapis Philosophicus"; the legendary substance said to turn base metals into gold and grant long life.</note> not be fluid like wax or oil? Why then do you say that, after the **solution** <term>Solution; the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state.</term> and **coagulation** <term>Coagulation; the process of a liquid thickening or becoming solid.</term>, it no longer has to fear such fluidity or damage? Perhaps your **medicine** <note>original: "medicina"; in alchemy, the "medicine" is the Stone itself, used to "heal" base metals into gold.</note> will be, like other worthless things, a hard and non-penetrating powder? 

Here one must note once again that after the completion of our work, the greatest and most secret science is required. For after our **Body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note> is properly **calcined** <term>Calcination; heating a substance to a high temperature to reduce it to a fine powder or ash.</term> with the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Mercurio Philosophorum"; a specialized alchemical solvent, distinct from common quicksilver.</note>, or dissolved and coagulated with the oft-mentioned **dry water** <note>original: "aqua sicca"; a paradoxically named alchemical solvent that does not wet hands.</note>, a separation finally occurs, so that the **dead earth** <note>original: "terra mortua"; the inert, useless residue left over after the vital essence has been extracted.</note> separates from the **Essence of the Sun** <note>original: "Essentia Solis"; the purified, spiritual energy or "soul" of gold.</note>, and must be separated, as **Sendivogius** <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist and court physician.</note> in his first treatise **On Mercury** <note>original: "de Mercurio".</note>...

<summary>This page explains that the final Philosopher's Stone is a perfect union of Spirit and Body that can no longer be separated by the elements. It clarifies a potential contradiction by explaining that a final separation of the "dead earth" from the "Essence of the Sun" is necessary to achieve the final, potent medicine.</summary>

# Of the Chemical Discourse #

-> L iii <-

Curio says: When it is burnt <note>Or "calcined"; reduced to ash by fire.</note>, one puts it into water, and afterwards one boils it. When it has been boiled, it is given to sick people, for which purpose I have already mentioned the **spirit of wine** <note>original: "Spiritum vini"; high-proof distilled alcohol used as a solvent to extract medicinal properties.</note> above. For this ***dead earth*** <note>original: "terra mortua"; the inert, solid residue left behind after the active principles have been extracted from a substance.</note>, being a worthless thing, possesses a great severity or hardness that hinders the **flow** <term>Flux; in alchemy, the ability of a substance to melt and penetrate other materials.</term> of our ***essence*** <note>original: "Essentiæ"</note>. 

But after the **separation** <term>Separation; the stage of the work where the pure, spiritual parts of a substance are divided from the impure, earthly dross.</term>, we possess the right, true, incombustible oil—the astral salt of the wise—the end which is like the beginning, the most worthy thing produced from the most unworthy. And in summary, after this final separation, you have your medicine in ***dry and liquid form*** <note>original: "forma sicca & liquida"</note>, the true treasure of all riches, the greatest secret that the Most High has hidden within nature, the true ***refuge of the deserted poor*** <note>original: "Asylon desertorum pauperum"</note>, and the right, true, and infallible ***increase*** <note>original: "augmentum"; the process by which the power or quantity of the Philosopher's Stone is multiplied.</note>. 

Indeed, it is such a treasure that I say you may cry out with **Basil** <note>Basil Valentine, a legendary 15th-century alchemist and monk whose works were central to the Hermetic tradition.</note>: **VICTORY**. For this salt has the victory over all things, over all volatile spirits of metals, and the power to harden **mercury** <note>original: "Mercurium"; the goal here is "fixation," turning liquid quicksilver into a solid, stable, and "fixed" metal like gold or silver.</note>.

# Second Part #

<page-num>168</page-num>

<margin>INTERNAL FIRE.</margin>
To speak somewhat of the hidden fire of our **matter** <note>original: "materiæ"</note>, I confess in truth that I must agree with the righteous **philosophers** <note>original: "Philosophis"</note> and say: that our **dry water** is nothing other than a sulfurous living fire. This is not, however, a common **sulfur** <note>original: "Sulphur"</note> which **calcines** <term>Calcination; the process of reducing a metal or mineral to a fine powder or ash through heat.</term>, burns, and destroys the metals. Rather, it is such a sulfurous water that leads the metals toward improvement. Concerning this, **Geber** <note>Jabir ibn Hayyan (c. 721–815), a foundational figure in alchemy known for his theories on the sulfur-mercury composition of metals.</note> said: *He who knows how to mix sulfur and make it friendly to bodies shall find one of the greatest secrets of nature.* <note>original: "Qui Sulphur commiscere & corporibus amicarinoverit, inveniet unū de secretis naturæ maximum."</note> This sulfurous living water is one of the greatest fires of all, which has the power to mix itself with our **body**. It is the true **essence** <note>original: "Essentia"</note> of the elemental, heavenly, and earthly fire, which mirrors the fire of the **Last Judgment** <note>original: "Jüngsten Gerichts"; the author compares the alchemical purification of matter to the biblical end of the world, where the earth is purified by fire.</note>, through which the **elements** <note>original: "Elementa"</note> themselves pass away, and new elements—being, as it were, the essences of the previous ones—shall be brought forth. However, because this so far exceeds the common understanding of men that it [is held] to be impossible <note>The text cuts off here; "impossible" is suggested by the catchword "müglich" (possible) on the following page.</note>

<summary>The author describes the "internal fire" of the alchemical matter as a "dry water" or "sulfurous living fire" that improves rather than destroys metals. He compares this transformative fire to the elemental purification of the world during the Last Judgment.</summary>

### of the Chemical Discourse.

...him, and in it are all things necessary for the art <note>original: "eum & in ea sunt omnia arti necessaria"</note>. Whoever teaches otherwise is a deceiver <note>original: "Sophist"; here meaning one who uses false arguments to mislead</note> and a pure, idle dreamer <note>original: "Grillenfänger"; literally a "cricket-catcher," a term for someone lost in whimsical or melancholy delusions</note>. Furthermore, if every philosopher <note>original: "Philosophus"</note> in the time of Bernhard <note>Bernhardus Trevisanus, the 15th-century alchemist mentioned on the previous page.</note> had made his stone <note>original: "lapidem"</note> from a different matter <note>original: "materia"</note>, no knowledge of them all would have followed so quickly. For it is precisely because they agreed in both word and deed that their knowledge and friendship <note>original: "amicitiam"</note> were brought about. Otherwise, everyone would have thought: this one worked <note>original: "laboriret"</note> in such a way, he is mistaken, it is a mere pretense, he did not use the correct matter <note>original: "materiam"</note>. But their concordance <note>original: "concordantz"</note> and consistent discourse <note>original: "discurs"</note> drew them together into the ground and foundation <note>original: "fundament"</note> of truth, so that they could judge whether this or that one walks within the proper bounds <note>original: "Schrancken"; referring to the barriers of a tournament or designated limits</note> of science.

# Of the Chymical Discourse.

...[di]vine **Providence** <note>original: "Providenz." The protective care of God or nature.</note> reveals only to the worthy; therefore, to prevent the Divine curse and the **mockery of Tantalus** <note>original: "Tantali Hohn." From Latin *scopus*, meaning the target or goal.</note> here, and will know how to take out and reduce something from the united bodies of the **Sun and Moon** <note>original: "Solis & Lunæ." Alchemical shorthand for Gold and Silver.</note> at the right time without further command.

If, however, he allows himself to be deluded into pursuing **particular works** <note>original: "particularia." From this it can be seen how the dear God does not reveal those things He has hidden within **Nature** <term>Natur: in this context, the creative force of the physical world which acts as a laboratory for God's hidden works.</term> to everyone; much less does He desire that such a high **mystery** <term>Mysterium: a divine secret or profound truth that transcends ordinary human understanding.</term>

### Third Part /

[...any] man to undertake anything in this matter; for among a thousand, there is hardly one faithful heart to be found that desires to keep faith and promise. In the 17th century, this term described a person's temperament or internal state of mind.</note>.

Therefore, every **student** <term>discipulus</term> of our chaste **Diana** <note>original: "Dianæ." In alchemical allegory, Diana (the goddess of the moon) represents the "Lunar" or silver stage of the work, emphasizing purity and the "white" stone.</note> is requested to diligently **observe** <term>observiren</term> what I have written, to **bid farewell** <term>valediciren</term> to all subtle **observations** <term>observationibus</term> <note>Here, "observations" refers to over-complicated or distracting theories.</note>, and rather to strive toward how he may wisely **prepare** <term>præpariren</term> the philosophical **subject** <term>subjectum</term> <note>The "subject" is the base material or "prime matter" used to begin the alchemical process.</note> and maintain it in the philosophical marriage bed <note>A metaphor for the hermetic vessel where the "male" and "female" principles of the matter are joined.</note> in proper **heat** <term>calore</term> <note>original: "calore." Precise control of heat was the most critical and difficult part of the alchemical process.</note> /

<summary>The author describes how the successful alchemist, granted success by God, renounces worldly greed and social vanity to focus on his spiritual legacy. He advises students to ignore complex theories and focus on the practical, careful heating of the alchemical "subject."</summary>

### of the Chemical Discourse. ###

[...to] hold, and may learn to await the harvest with patience. In the meantime, let his prayer and patience be firm and constant; do not deviate from one once-perceived possibility to another, but follow Nature, strive to fear GOD, to love one’s neighbor, and to be ready at all times to give an account to GOD in Heaven. Guard yourself, on the other hand, against all those who have no other **foundations** <term>fundamenta</term> than those described above; let him take an example from those who profess nothing but absurd things, such that they hold their **pine and fir resin** to be the true **Subject** <term>Subjectum</term> <note>original: "Subjectum," the base material from which the Work begins.</note>, and for that reason allow themselves to be given several hundred **Thalers** <note>A large silver coin used throughout Europe.</note> alongside the **oath** <term>juramento</term> and **seal of silence** <term>Sigillo silentii</term> merely for the naming of the same. They do this in the opinion that no closer **matter** <term>materia</term> and no other gum of our **Philosophical Stone** exists, because the deceptive **rods** <note>This likely refers to dowsing rods or perhaps "divining rods" used by frauds to "prove" the presence of alchemical secrets in common materials.</note> were struck upon it, wherein the **Philosophers' Dragon** <term>Philosophorum Drach</term> <note>original: "Philosophorum Drach." ### Third Part / ###

[...rods refer to a] common water, which is poor, and many other **gums** <term>gummata</term> <note>original: "gummata," refers to resins or plant exudates used in chemical experiments.</note> brought from the Eastern and Southern lands, which far surpass common resin. He <note>The student or seeker of alchemy.</note> should avoid all large **compositions** <term>Compositiones</term> <note>original: "Compositiones," complex chemical mixtures or multi-ingredient recipes often used by frauds to confuse the buyer.</note>, and let himself imagine no **particular recipe** <term>particular</term> <note>original: "particular," an alchemical process that claims to transmute only a small portion of metal or produce a specific limited result rather than the "Universal" Stone.</note>, because common silver can be **improved** <term>melioriret</term> <note>original: "melioriret," meaning to refine, enhance, or "ripen" a metal into a higher state.</note> in no other way than through the goodness of gold. He should consider it as mere borrowed babble <note>original: "Waschwerck," literally "laundry-work," an idiom used here to mean nonsensical talk or hearsay.</note> that no gold exists unless it was first silver, and no silver comes to be where it was not previously lead, tin, and copper. For if this were true, **royal water** <term>aqua Regis</term> <note>original: "aqua Regis," a powerful mixture of acids capable of dissolving gold.</note> would dissolve silver as well as gold, and common **strong water** <term>aqua fort</term> <note>original: "aqua fortis," or nitric acid, which dissolves silver but leaves gold intact</note> would **dissolve** <term>solviren</term> gold as well as silver, and the **particular recipes** <term>particularia</term> would not be so costly. For we would not need to look long for a **Tincture** <term>Tinctur</term> <note>original: "Tinctur," the medicine or agent used to perform transmutation.</note>; it would be a simple matter for lead, copper, or silver ore to be **digested** <term>digerirt</term> <note>original: "digerirt," the process of exposing a substance to gentle, consistent heat over time.</note> and **transmuted** <term>transmutirt</term> into a better metal—as if such a possibility could be brought further in the **alchemists'** furnaces in one year than **Nature's cook** <note>A traditional alchemical personification of the natural, slow heat within the Earth that "ripens" metals over vast spans of time.</note> in the earth is able to do in 1,000 years—

<summary>The author warns against complex, fraudulent recipes and the false theory that metals must naturally "evolve" from lead to silver to gold, arguing that alchemical shortcuts cannot realistically replicate Nature's thousand-year processes.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, transmutation, aqua regis, aqua fortis, tincture, particularia, digestion, Nature's cook, gums, silver refinement</keywords>

I remain, finally, of the opinion that every **genus** <term>genus</term> <note>original: "genus," meaning a specific class or category of substance.</note> has its own **seed** <term>Saamen</term> <note>original: "Saamen," the internal "blueprint" or seminal power that dictates what a substance will become.</note> and cannot be improved except through the excessive **fixity** <term>Fixigkeit</term> <note>original: "Fixigkeit," the quality of being "fixed," or resistant to being vaporized by heat; a hallmark of noble metals like gold.</note> of gold and silver. On the other hand, I allow it to be true that the beginning of metals is generally a **vitriolic salty water** <term>Victriolisch Salzhafftig Wasser</term>, which is **impregnated** by the **sulfur** <term>Sulphure</term> <note>original: "Sulphure," referring here to the alchemical principle of "soul" or combustibility, rather than just the yellow mineral.</note> of each respective kind, and is thereafter transformed into a metal according to what that sulfur is. 

Now, if someone wanted to **resolve** <term>resolviren</term> <note>original: "resolviren," to break down a solid metal back into its original liquid or "prime" state.</note> the lesser metals into their first water through the first water, and afterwards wanted to impregnate and **fix** <term>figiren</term> <note>original: "figiren," the alchemical process of making a substance stable and non-volatile.</note> them with the seed of gold and silver: who would live to see the outcome of such **labor** <note>original: "laboris," Latin for "work" or "toil."</note>, and who could find the "first water" of the metals? 

Let everyone take warning and follow my teaching; then he will perceive that his work is well applied. I will also then take cause to bring my good thoughts further to light. Meanwhile, may Almighty GOD grant to the...

### Third Part of the **Chemical Discourse**

--- Page 220 ---
It seems to me that the first and second parts intend to say the following:
Take <term>mercury</term> <note>original: "Quecksilber," also known as quicksilver</note>, distill it with antimony metal or, preferably, arsenic metal until both have combined into a single mercury. Then <term>amalgamate</term> <note>the process of combining a metal with mercury to form a soft paste or liquid alloy</note> gold and also silver with it; bring both amalgams together into a glass flask.

<image-desc>A simple line drawing of a long-necked glass flask (retort or phial) with a rounded base containing a shaded liquid or substance.</image-desc>

And place it in sand-heat <note>a sand bath, which provides a steady, indirect heat</note>. Initially keep it at about 80° warm for one month; then it will turn black <note>the "nigredo" stage, symbolizing decomposition</note>. Then increase the fire, and many colors will appear <note>often called the "peacock’s tail" in alchemical literature</note>. After 4 months, increase the fire again, and the substance will slowly become white <note>the "albedo" stage of purification</note>. After 5 months, increase the heat and it will turn red <note>the "rubedo" stage, representing the final perfection of the work</note>. The red substance must be mixed again with the mercury, and so it will again turn black, white, red, etc. <note>a process known as "multiplication," intended to increase the power of the substance</note>.

<summary>This page provides a practical summary of an alchemical procedure for creating a "red" substance (likely the Philosopher's Stone) through the timed heating and color-transformation of a mercury-gold-silver amalgam.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, mercury, antimony, arsenic, gold, silver, sand bath, nigredo, albedo, rubedo, distillation, transmutation</keywords>
